In the area of economic and development, he announced concrete measures, including a reduction in taxation and contributions, as we were committed to, which are currently on the table by households, professionals and businesses. It is imperative to create new, real, well-paid jobs, which will come through the release of the creative forces of the Greeks and the encouragement of investment. It is imperative that the development projects and the interventions which had been frozen for five years should be implemented. Here I also convey Argolida's request, according to which works crucial for the development and quality of life, such as the great irrigation of Anavalos, the new safe connection between Argolida and Corinth-Tripolios and Kalamata motorway, the road network, the works at the port and the Nafplio marina, as well as cultural works such as the Archaeological Museum of Argos and Burgis, whether they were frozen by the previous government or faced unjustified delays. It is imperative that the primary sector, the farmer and the farmer whose cost of production is greater than the sales prices of the products should be substantially supported. There is an urgent need for substantial support for tourism and industry in order to compete with the tourist product. Finally, it is now imperative that the state should be resolutely restructured so that it operates effectively as a citizen's helper and not as a force, a state which is flexible and functional that, for example, does not charge the agricultural world with unjustified bureaucratic obligations, free trade, the small and medium-sized enterprises and, above all, a state that ensures legality for everyone everywhere without exceptions, without unjustified illegality and infringibility. With its first steps, the new government of Kyriakos Mitsotakis and the New Democracy demonstrates our determination to do the great quality change that our citizens need and that our country needs. In the face of sincerity, seriousness, responsibility, efficiency, the conscious pursuit of national unity and collective good we are called upon not only to bring home after ten years of harsh crisis, not only to ensure a greater prospect for society and the national economy, but much more to launch a new historic period for Greece, a new period of regularity, stability and progress, self-confidence and pride for each Greek and every Greek. I am sure that once again the great liberal party will respond successfully to its national duty. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u39	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, let me begin by thanking my fellow citizens at the Elias Prefecture once again for their vote. I hope I'm worthy of their trust. I also hope that we all have a good and constructive term for the benefit of the Greek people. Our work this time is important, primarily because we will be called upon to exercise the highest legislative power, namely to revise the Constitution, but also because we will be given the opportunity to legislate on a number of issues and to lead our country back to growth and normality. Our aim should be to lead our fellow citizens to the exit from the deadlocks brought to them by the country's previous governance. Today the Greeks can be optimistic again. A new page has already begun for our country, as, as we learn from the Programming Declarations, a programme of development and good governance is beginning to be implemented quickly, efficiently and rationally. The country's Prime Minister, inspired by the principles of good governance, good fiscal policy, liberal and social policy, at the same time, summarized the government's programme in his speech, aiming at strong growth in a self-powered Greece, a Greece that finally exploits and relies on its own powers. Strong growth is based on the growth of enterprises through the reduction of business tax, support for their liquidity and regulation of red loans and other debts, and new, additional jobs will be created to tackle the long-term unemployment that is stirring up our many fellow citizens today. The reduction of income tax and other tax reliefs, which are already starting in 2019, are measures that will give our fellow citizens a breath, but in the local and national economy. Support for family and socially vulnerable groups and strengthening health and education is at the heart of our social policy. In order to raise our economy, we will emphasise, among other things, agricultural and livestock production, which, as we have just heard, aims to strengthen agricultural incomes with respect to the natural environment and to exploit high technology, but also to tourism. In order to strengthen, however, tourism in areas such as Western Greece, we must complete our transport infrastructure. For Elia it is an absolute priority to complete the construction of the Olympia Road, Patras - Tower and the improvements of the Tower - Good Water. I would remind you that in 2014 we had legislated on the completion of the Olympian road and set a legal timetable for its completion in time. Instead, however, the SYRIZA government has decided to take the road that we had opened for it and to finish the work at its specified time, and it has decided to break the project into eight subcontractors and the four of them will go to the "TOXOTIS" company of Mr. Kalogrica. This company has so far failed to respond to the work it has undertaken and the construction of the road has not even begun. ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u117	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to speak to you today as a scientist for their hospitals and their problems. Only one pathologist serves at Lemnos Hall and on-calls thirty days. I did it 30 years ago. There's no oncologist, so that there's no chemo in Lemnos, the one our neighbors really want. We know very well, ladies and gentlemen, who is being put directly, who has a direct impact and who is not one of the ordinary citizens, who is looking for hospital treatment in the area of public hospitals more and more because of the crisis and is entitled to receive the best possible services. I will also address the burning issue of the unbearable costs of insurance and care for refugees and migrants entering our country, which is a cost specifically borne by Greek taxpayers. First of all, the drug is an essential asset. It is the only way to ensure our health and better life. There is no doubt that the Greek state must intervene and support the Greek pharmaceutical industry by investing in research and, in particular, in the herbs that we have, as a country, over a thousand eight hundred species. Research, production, processing, processing and disposal of Greek products will give our young people jobs, not allowances that deprive them of dignity. We must then invest in the production of essential oils, which will be used in the production of medicines and at the same time in the production of cosmetics. The porridge that thrives on the northern continent is sold with 50,000 euros a kilo. Certainly, the proper design of the production of pharmaceutical herbs also has the effect of promoting the activation of high added-value products, as competition is limited to the number of herbs produced. This also works for the benefit of farmers, namely, of our farmers. The pharmaceutical industry contributes significantly to our country's total external trade, since in 2014 exports of medicinal products approached one billion and constituted the second export product in terms of value. If we can re-establish the pharmaceutical industry, then we will have less chance of finding ourselves in the face of cases such as 'NOVARTIS' which, in order to promote their own medicines and products, seem to have carried out procedures which, perhaps, were not the right ones. The collapse of our pharmaceutical industry, which has been active for sixty years because of the troika and the constant price rises, has adversely affected the production of medicines. Our aim is to get out of the vicious circle of deficits and overcompensation, reducing the imports of foreign medicines by increasing our Greek counterparts and our exports. The pharmaceutical industry is a key investment lever in Greece. With the expenditure on research, the development of new therapies can be an important core of employment for the children of the Greek family, so the Greek Health Funding Solution proposes a number of measures, some of which relate to the exploitation of drug-related resources. First of all, determining VAT on 6% medicine. This amount, resulting from the tax, will be allocated directly to the Ministry of Health for the financing and payment of the needs of hospitals, the on-calls of medical staff and overtime of nurses and paramedical staff, as well as for the supply of medical, technological equipment. Secondly, incentives and promotion of actions, research and development for the production of domestic medicinal products for domestic consumption and for export. Thirdly, we are proposing to set up a national pharmaceutical industry to provide additional incentives for private pharmaceutical industries with specific low-cost incentives and with tax exemptions to stay in our country and to operate by giving jobs to scientific and other staff. The share capital contribution for medicines is to be determined at 10% for most categories. The category of medicines includes medicinal herbs, therefore, it is proposed to establish a crop programme for these herbs by the Ministry of Health in cooperation with the Ministry of Rural Development, where farmers will have a reduced income tax than others. These herbs will be used for the domestic manufacture of preparations and for export as raw material treated or raw. Sixthly, the implementation of our strategic plan for the use of pharmaceutical herbs and aromatic plants in the production of high quality cosmetics. Greece is the only country in the world that, because of the climate and the proper geophysical conditions, can grow such crops, because there are thousands of recorded pharmaceutical and aromatic plants, such as, say, the aloe ring, grown in Greece and have global success. We urge you to do this by respecting the wishes of the majority of the Greek people who condemn and oppose this agreement. Thank you.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u118	Thank you, Mrs. Athanasios. Mr Thomas, Secretary of State for Environment and Energy, has the floor for seven minutes. Then Mr. Friends will speak according to the list. Mr. Undersecretary, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u119	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, this is the first time I have spoken. The Government commits itself to a national energy policy that will ensure the country's energy security, with the aim of gradually reducing greenhouse gas emissions in the shortest possible time. We are committed to national energy policy being socially fair. By the end of the year, as provided for in European legislation, Greece's national energy and climate plan, which we want to be ambitious in terms of renewable energy, energy saving, innovation and new technologies, will be submitted. Our energy policy will be compatible with the temperature targets of the Paris Agreement and the long-term European sustainable development strategy. Our aim is for the energy transition to take place in such a way that it attracts investment, to benefit to the maximum from Community funds and the resources of the European Investment Bank, to acquire extroversion and to bring about growth by creating new jobs. Of course, the first urgent priority is PPC and the treatment of its ill economic situation, which was presented to us on the first day by the management of the company, which "ERNST & YOUNG" has publicly presented as a sworn accountant. There will be rationalisation interventions, structural measures to promote the reorganisation of PPC’s overall market position, but also to ensure that sources of development are provided for the future PPC, such as RES, which PPC has not invested in the last four years and has a market share of less than 4%. But that is why Mr Hatzidakis will be speaking in detail on Monday. In Greece, the electricity market has remained a long way behind, especially in terms of liberalisation and competition. That is why we are launching areas for strengthening the credibility of the institutions and other market players, which will open up competition, we are creating conditions for the energy stock exchange to function as soon as possible and for the target model to be adopted, which has been delayed, although it is an unsuccessful obligation for the European Union. Thus, we will achieve growth, competition, integration and cooperation with neighbouring countries and ultimately low prices for consumers, which is something we all want. We are taking steps to strengthen the competitiveness of our country's energy-intensive industry and small and medium-sized enterprises. With regard to electricity infrastructure we give priority to the major interconnection projects, Crete - Attiki, Dodecanese - Crete, Ikaria - Samos - Cyclades, North Aegean, and to the integration of the Cyclades with mainland Greece. In this way we support island Greece and the country's tourism industry and we strengthen our activities within the framework of the 'Natural Energy for the islands of Europe' initiative. At the same time we are already planning the transition, adaptation and financial support of the regions of Greece that will be most affected by gradual decarbonisation. Our strategy is clearly aimed at reducing energy poverty, with a view to social cohesion, alleviating the weak social households and, more generally, the economic benefit of the final consumer. We are committed to the rapid development of the market in renewable energy, given our country's geographical location, with the aim of overcoming the EIA penetration targets in the energy mix set by the European Union and making investments. The Greek Thinking Reservoir for the Energy Economy appreciates investment of 10 billion in the RES by 2030. We're starting with the simplification of the license. The average licensing time is now seven years, when the European benchmark is two years. In order to get there we need to shorten with the time of inspection and approval, to automate procedures by applying an electronic management system, by digital transfer of files and by digital opinion of all the competent authorities. This will be done together with the revision of the specific spatial planning and sustainable development, launched by the previous government and that will accelerate. We will form a clear institutional framework for extending the working time of these RES parks and starting a strategic study of a specific framework for wind parks offshore. At the new age of electricity generation we will encourage investments in energy netting from households and businesses, promote a decentralised energy-sharing system, which will support the sale of electricity to the network by small producers, whether they are farmers or small or simple citizens. In this direction it is necessary to introduce as soon as "smart meters", which will, by the way, help effectively and to combat the rhetoric. Energy storage, which can convert RES energy into stored fuel, will be found in the ultimate priority of investment and research funds that we manage national and Community funds. We will develop mature storage solutions, such as hydroelectric, and new technologies, such as modern batteries and hydrogen. We support the development of biomass and biogas plants, as well as high enthusiasm geothermals. At the same time, we will also take initiatives on low enthusiasm with the other ministries. Energy saving and active efficiency can be an important pillar of development for the country. Our aim is to continue the program of "Home-to-house" with the rationalisation of the terms, so as to benefit as many of our fellow citizens as possible, gradually reaching the hundreds of thousands of homes a year. It will facilitate certification, lead the domestic construction and manufacturing industry, achieve more and more substantial leverage from the banking system and programmes such as ‘Invest EU’ and the European Investment Bank, while addressing tax exemptions in case of the implementation of energy upgrading measures. Similar programmes are designed for small and medium-sized enterprises. An important role will be called upon to play the public, which will show exemplary action. Energy renovation in public buildings will take place not only through the NSRP, whose resources are not sufficient, but also through the purchase of energy services and energy efficiency contracts. In the transport sector, emphasis will be placed on promoting electricity and developing green public transport. The Prime Minister has already announced a commitment yesterday to renew public transport in four years' time. In the gas sector, we will reorganise the PPA so that they can obtain the supply of gas in terms as competitive as possible for consumers. Our aim is to make the necessary revisions of the law of the previous government so that the project of privatization will yield and be profitable to the public. We are committed to the timely implementation of long-term infrastructure projects, which will help Greece deepen its strategic links. We give priority to investing the natural gas networks in urban areas and we will examine the possibilities of investing LNG in the large ports of the country for the purposes of shipping.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u132	In 2014, how old was it?
ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u133	That's the problem for the country for decades. But now, because we are talking about the 4th Industrial Revolution, we have to go over and start as a ministry by setting an ambitious objective: in four years we have converged with the European Union's average. We started with a change. The Ministry we took over is not the same as the Ministry we received. First of all, communication is not part of the new Ministry. It's never supposed to be. Secondly, we accumulated the diverse responsibilities of the state involved in IT there. The first task is to simplify procedures. You can't just publish digital works. You should also simplify procedures. Technology exists to make citizens' lives better. There's not just about digitizing red tape. For this reason, the old powers related to simplifications and which until recently were located in the old Ministry of Administrative Reform are now in the new digital ministry, along with the JEPs. In this respect, the new Ministry will take the initiative to develop a national framework for simplifying procedures and reducing bureaucracy, which among others will include the creation of a register of administrative procedures for the public, which will be accessible to citizens so that they can know what procedures are in place and what case they are - and in this context we will also work with all the ministries - and the launch of a horizontal programme for measuring and reducing administrative burdens on the legislation, corresponding to what had been run in 2014 by the then Minister for Administrative Reform and today's Prime Minister together with the OECD. The JEPs are one of the most successful institutions of the Greek state. If you see relevant research, it is the most popular entity of the Greek state. However, in recent years the JEPs have been neglected. We are bringing this excellent institution back to the fore. We are strengthening it and making use of it. And in this context we will make a reassessment of all the services that the JEPs offer today, we will reconvene their operating framework to modernise them and upgrade their infrastructure to the standards of the best corresponding private infrastructure. One of the first actions we will also be taking - and we have talked about this in detail in the tradition - the receipt of the Ministry - is about the so-called life-threatening events of the citizens. Each of us from the moment of birth until the time of the loss of a loved one's life experiences a series of events. And each event entails bureaucratic action. Each event involves tails. When a child is born, there are too many of the tails. Why are these tails? Because the various systems of the Greek state, the various registers do not work together. They don't talk to each other. That is why one of our first actions will be to take one - one of these events of life and try to simplify them. Where there are five tails to become one or two. Especially for birth, we think we will have achieved this in very few months. In this context, we will use and transform the General Secretariat of Information Systems. Until first, the General Secretariat of Information Systems was an entity of the Ministry of Finance. Now there will be a General Secretariat of Information Systems of the government, all over the country, a public administration data centre. And to that end we will extend the so-called Jiggle out infrastructure so that all the data will be gradually transferred there to enable the interoperability that I have spoken about. Of course this must be done, especially as regards digital projects, in the context of a broader project. In the Ministry we will promote a digital transformation paper in the country. The Bible will be the practical guide defining the principles and implementation plan of the digital strategy and setting measurable targets and measurable results per quarter. The Bible will be updated annually through an open and cooperative process with both the other ministries and with the citizens in consultation in order to meet the needs of the State and the public administration concerned. It will respond to the fragmentation of competences for digital governance, the overlapping or fragmented actions and the lack of measurable targeting in the entities. We will start with the short-term actions of three to twelve months, like the events I mentioned before, namely, the cutting of the tails. At the same time, we will also raise a site by imitating a very good example of the United Kingdom. It's called gov.uk. We will build a corresponding portal for Greece, which will be a central point of entry and digital service where all digital solutions and everything that citizens and other traders should know with the public are concentrated. It will rise quickly and we will increase the data that will gradually contain, in the depth of the four-year period. The medium-term natural interventions concern in fact - because we are talking about technology - major projects. And with regard to large projects, if you look at it in its detail, there are projects which have been unresolved for ten years, such as the CRM, the service of the traders. Human resources management. HRMS is the arctic. If you see presentations by Greek governments of 2010 or 2011, these projects are there. And they haven't been proclaimed yet. Our aim is to make the relevant announcements soon and by the end of the year, to have published these works, but also several more of which we need. Networks and telecommunications. Since the first days of the new government's life - theory of Halkidiki, the Attica earthquake - we remembered that telecommunications is a basic human need. ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u153	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, Greek shipping represents the most extroverted sector of our national economy and one of the most important factors of our country's power, since it produces wealth, maintains thousands of jobs and gives strategic prestige to our country. I am here today to present to you the strategy, objectives and key priorities of the Ministry of Shipping and Island Policy. In the maritime sector, the Greek merchant fleet is the largest in the world with four thousand nine hundred and thirty-six ships, with a capacity of three hundred and ninety million dead Weight. It is first in the world at a capacity of about 21%, first of all in the European Union at a rate of 53%. The Greek register numbered seven hundred twenty ships, about 15% on all Greek vessels. Of course, he is second in the European Union and eighth in the world. At the same time as the Greek fleet is developing, the Greek register is constantly falling and this is done in an international environment where there are wider geopolitical and geostrategic changes. Shipping is called upon to face major challenges in the international regulatory framework, in IT technologies where the landscape of shipping is changing, in the fluctuations in the market, in the problem of maritime finances. International competition, trade wars, new environmental data, new environmental regulations which will apply from 1-1-2020. And we all realise that something must obviously be changed with the Greek register. In order to achieve this objective, it needs to be substantially upgraded to the standards of organisation and functioning of the main competitions. For us the issues of simplification and digitisation will be matters of immediate priority. However, the register of the new era that we are visionary is not an end in itself, it is part of an integrated maritime policy which takes into account the improvement of the conditions of employability of Greek seafarers and the upgrading of maritime education. For us, the upgrading of public maritime education is a first priority. We all have to realise that maritime education is directly linked, but also decisively, not only to the preservation, but also to the strengthening of the know-how of the Greek seafarers, what we all recognize here and outside as the shipping of the Greeks. We are therefore moving forward in improving and modernising the existing regulatory framework governing maritime education by introducing a framework law for the organisation and operation of the whole of it. In other words, for the first time, we are dealing with issues of public maritime education. We also proceed to the immediate activation and operation of the KESEN of New Machinery in Macedonia, to the qualitative and quantitative upgrading of the human capital of our maritime education, both at the level of trainers and at the level of trainers. Modernising public maritime education will come from support for logistical infrastructure, internationalisation of curricula and, of course, from ensuring stable and experienced educational staff. Strategic selection and central objective of the Mitsotakis Government is a new architecture of the maritime education system in Greece. We are essentially supporting and investing in sustainability, modernisation, upgrading the pillar of public maritime education. We want public maritime education strong and we will achieve it. At the same time, however, our aim is to implement a new institutional framework for the promotion of private maritime education, in other words, in all modern internationally competitive shipping centres, the same practical practice to apply in our country, always within the framework of the service and benefit of the Greek seafarer and the Greek shipping industry. In our ports, in the field of port policy, we must realise that our ports can and must develop into the steam engines of the national and regional economy that are part of a single transport and supply system. To highlight these comparative advantages, a direct change in the country's port policy is required. To this end, we will move rapidly towards updating the national port strategy, taking into account the objectives, of course, of regional development. Our priorities are as follows: For the ten major regional ports we will, in each port separately, proceed to an assessment in viability studies, of course, in cooperation with the TEAD. We will also make it possible to leverage funding through the mobilisation of public, private and Community resources. We will speed up the completion of investment and development projects at the ports of Piraeus and Thessaloniki and will, finally, proceed - to rationalise the system of public bodies and structures dealing with ports. As far as marine tourism is concerned, we are investing and we will invest too much. So in this crucial area, which will take a decisive role, taking legislative initiatives, we will move forward in modernising and simplifying the institutional framework for the operation of pleasure boats, we will digitise procedures and, of course, we will improve its competitiveness. In other words, we will copy standards successful by other competing countries in the field of maritime tourism. We are also going on to restructure and solve problems in the vessel register so that it becomes a useful tool for national maritime tourism policy and not just a tool for non-developmental overtaxation, as has been the case so far. In other words, we want open seas for all, but under rules and respect for national and Community law. In the cruise sector, in cooperation with the relevant ministries and particularly with the Ministry of Tourism, we will do everything necessary to extend the home porting to the Greek ports and to involve companies in the industry's value chain. With regard to the Port House: Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, in March 2019 the Port House completed a hundred years of offer at the site. The Port Corps does not belong to the Ministry of Shipping and Island Policy. The Port House is the Ministry of Shipping and Island Policy itself, and it is the unadulterated pillar of its efforts in every area of its competence. The Port House offers excellent services and effectively attempts throughout its area of responsibility with decisive actions such as research and rescue, surveillance and conservation of maritime borders, combating all illegal acts at sea, protecting the marine environment and, of course, maritime safety. It is also important to offer the Port House to the critical sectors of Merchant Shipping, such as representation in international shipping forums where Greece must and must have a leading role. Our aim, of course, is to strengthen the operational capacity of the Port Authority by ensuring the appropriate means, as well as the incorporation into its ranks, of the sense of security and recognition of its important and multi-level work. For these reasons, not a single euro should be lost for the Port House armament programme, for which money has been secured by the previous New Democracy Government - more than EUR 200 million - by mobilising resources from the NSRF, the European Union's Internal Security Fund and other financial instruments. National programmes, such as ERASH, the integrated maritime surveillance system in the south-eastern Aegean, obviously and should proceed quickly. They're late. In any case, however, they should proceed as soon as possible. And, of course, we will be preparing for the next 2021-2027 programming period with an integrated programme to strengthen the services of the Port Authority. In the maritime sector, we must be clear and clear. Coastal fishing contributes decisively to the cohesion of our national area. Our priority is to develop and upgrade the existing ferry network, while, of course, modernising the ferry fleet, always with a view to assessing modern transport data and addressing the challenges it will face with the implementation of the new environmental data from 2020 onwards. It is not a political innovation to talk about shaping and adopting a public policy on the island area. It is a constitutional check, and that is what we have to do. All we need to remember is that 18.7% of the total surface area of the country's territory consists of islands where there is a population of about 15.1% of the country's total population. In the context of the immediate priorities of the Ministry of Shipping and Island Policy, it is the three-fold maritime economy - blue growth - islands as the building block of our island policy. Immediate priorities in the field of island policy are, therefore, the coordination of a national strategy for blue development, maintenance and extension of implementation of the insularity clause in draft legislation, assessment and rationalisation of transport equivalency, while adopting incentives or aid to improve island competitiveness. The insularity compensation should improve the competitiveness of enterprises and not create subsidised dependent economies. Finally, there will be a promotion of modern financial instruments for the implementation of island investments with leverage to implement necessary projects, transport infrastructure, energy, environment, water management, in the light of the cyclical economy, while at the same time seeking to mobilise funds directly through integrated territorial investments of the Structural Funds for the period 2021-2027. Ladies and gentlemen, the unilateral and in many ways ideological approach of the previous government to the issues of shipping, but also of island policy, has led to the country's loss of valuable time and strength. We, as the Prime Minister, Mr Mitsotakis, made it clear yesterday, do not have the luxury, but do not choose the policy of umbilical review. Greece must have the first say in the planning of the maritime agenda at world level. I am sure our country will play its part as a peaceful force for progress and development in the wider Southeast Mediterranean region. Thank you very much.

Ladies and gentlemen, in order to deal effectively with the difficult problem of immigration, the country urgently needs a coherent and comprehensive strategy that starts from the deterrent, goes to the protection and management of borders, reaches the granting or non-asylum, and ultimately reaches the integration policies of those who are legally staying in the country. A strategy that will be developed both internally and abroad, but also with action measures at parallel levels: legislative, operational level, level of infrastructure and human resources, public diplomacy, in order for the country to transmit the right messages internally and abroad. Our policy begins and is defined by a fundamental assumption. In managing this complex issue, every democratic state must take into account two interrelated factors: respect for human rights on the one hand and respect for and defending the good of security on the other. Our aim is to establish a political and operational link between these two principles. The indifference and devaluation of security - the practice in Greece has shown - is ultimately at the expense of human rights. Moria, Vathy and several years ago the unacceptable image at the old airport of Greek and Danish icon Icon. The ideological relaxation eventually crushes human dignity, offends the rights of both those who come and the rights of the Greeks who live in their homeland and seek safety in an environment of social peace in order to develop economic, personal, business. So against the ideological slackness that undermines rights, we are proposing the democratic rigour that respects rights and rules. In particular, our policy is made up of a number of objectives and actions. On the inside, strengthening the borders, strengthening the preservation of the common Greek and European borders, in particular seas with a new integrated plan, upgrading the necessary infrastructure and strengthening the human resources mandate, where necessary, at the entry and exit points of the country. In this context, our cooperation with FRONTEX and the International Organisation for Migration must be stimulated and given greater substance. By the end of the year, the legal basis and the legal framework for granting asylum is reformed to examine the asylum seekers' applications and to deal with them quickly so that those who have the right to know it quickly and those who illegally entered the country know that they will enter a fragile and decisive return programme. In the same way, we are also stepping up the appeal committees to a second degree and deepening our cooperation with the EASO and the UN High Commissioner for Refugees. Also, there is the will to implement a firm and effective return schedule for those who do not have the right to stay in Greece. In the previous period this process by the previous government was characterised by a very low rate of implementation, so that the country loses credibility when it went to Europe and asked for solidarity and support. ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u218	Mr Karagunis is not in the Chamber, so we will continue with Minister Theocharis. Mr. Theochari, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u219	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I begin with something we all agree with: Tourism is the country's heavy industry. Its success is the result of collective and continuous effort of workers, individuals and state. This has proved to be awkward in the years of the crisis. From 2009 to 2018, tourism has directly produced 125 billion euros, with multiple contributions of course, to economic activity and to GDP as a whole, while 230 million people have been hosted, have travelled to our country. In other words, the crisis would be much worse, much harder, without tourism. At the same time, however, over the last ten years, the number of tourists has increased more than the tourist income has increased. That's proof the state didn't practice the right politics. It has simply followed the juncture, which has indeed favoured our country, but which has come to an end and we must know that. If we do not take initiatives, if we do not manage to increase tourist GDP, while keeping arrivals stable, we will have a problem, because that should be our goal, every tourist should be longer days and leave more money. This is why the Government of New Democracy proposes incentives to improve the quality of hotels, in exchange for more common spaces. That is how we will achieve these goals. But let us come to today and, above all, to tomorrow. For us, tourism is not just numbers. Tourism means hospitality, with emphasis on humans and, of course, on the experience it experiences. Tourism for the Ministry's leadership, but also for the New Democracy Government as a whole, means strategy. Firstly, a strategy for sustainable tourism development. It means, for example, a balance of supply, demand and infrastructure, not a power cut every weekend, as I was told in Halkidiki I was going to do, that before the premise of every weekend they had a power cut or a waste that cannot be collected, as is the case in Corfu, which still has a real problem. Secondly, it means avoiding overtourism. This year, on August 6, they will reach Santorini cruise ships with a capacity of eighteen thousand three hundred and seventy-two. The island can't stand so many people. Is it the people who will protest that day? Is it the tourists who won't come back? We cannot call a tourist product the suffering of visitors and residents. These are the results of an anarchist and without strategic planning of tourist development. Thirdly, it means a data analysis of who we want to attract, when we want them and why. In the era of the great data, the so-called 'big data', we cannot make marketing with intuition. It needs analysis, innovation and a new EOT, adapted to new conditions and new tools in its hands. We are therefore engraving a new digital strategy at the EOT. Fourthly, it means quality accommodations and groups with a low environmental footprint and why not, zero carbon footprint. The Scandinavian countries are first, but this trend - we are all sure and you know it - will be extended over the next few years. They only choose green destinations. We can't ignore that. Ladies and gentlemen, the strategic objectives of our Ministry are summarised as follows: Improving the competitiveness of the Greek tourist product, improving its quality and authenticity, improving the durability and finally improving the sustainability of the tourist product. In order to achieve all this, we need better coordination between the ministries, but also of course between state and local government. We will pursue this firstly by setting up a team to implement the actions of the national tourism plan. Secondly, with the establishment of a tourism council, so that tourist counter-regionals meet regularly with the Ministry's leadership, to better coordinate actions across the country. Ladies and gentlemen, five are the axes on which we will move: the creation of infrastructure, the improvement of the licensing framework and the placement of tourism, such as the completion of the spatial framework. It is a matter of years or decades, in order to favour investment, without a discount on the principles of sustainability, by creating a one-stop shop for the rapid licensing of tourist investments, which has been inactivated, by standardising environmental studies for new tourist facilities, where things are simpler and can be done, with incentives for the registration of professional and private boats. The aim is to attract five thousand vessels more than ten metres which currently go to other states. Today, for example, Croatia is taking it to us and we are losing too much revenue as a result. The second axis is the country's accessibility and interconnectivity, with an effort to strengthen the country's air and other interconnections, such as direct flights from the United States and winter, with an ambitious plan to extend and upgrade the marinas - the Prime Minister said yesterday - to improve the services offered, by connecting all tourist and business on the internet, with quality websites to enhance their active digital presence. The third axis is the management of the experience so that it is qualitative and attractive. The experience of a visitor starts from the beginning when he thinks about the destination and is looking to learn more about it, passes through the journey itself, through the tail to the customs, the quality of the room, if he was stolen in the street and what the police did when it happened to him and, of course, ends with the management of his memories and social networks, which usually raises them. We will strengthen the role of tourist police, with a daily presence for the transmission of the feeling of security to all tourists and entrepreneurs. We will reintroduce technical tourism education from almost zero and upgrade the upper schools of tourism. The country is a world-class tourist destination. It is not meant to have world-class tourism training. These two go together. Because only in this way will tourism workers become more qualitative, more productive and, of course, better paid. The prosperity of tourism will be diffused to everyone. Fourth axis is the redesign of the tourist product and the redefinition of its image, what we say, in greek, rebranding. To do this, we need evidence. We are the only country that has not yet implemented the tourism account in ELSTAT. This will change and change quickly. With the immediate reintroduction of our tourism competitiveness by reducing VAT on packages from 24% to 13% and over a period of four years at 11%. In order to achieve the expansion of the tourist season, we will call on all players per region, so that coordinated actions can create special winter products, so that we can bring people and other seasons here. We will be opening up - in short - our tourism in the five senses and not just in the sun and in the sea, as is happening today. The visitor must leave having seen, heard, taste, smell, touch from our country. This can be done by turning into composite or alternative products, city break, medical, well-being tourism, sport, educator, religious, gastronomic, conference, rural tourism and more. Other countries have already made a great deal of progress in this area and we are very much back in this. We will actively support efforts that create new attractive destinations in undeveloped tourist areas. Specific actions will be designed for cultural and religious tourism, which is the second largest market in Europe in expenditure and the third in arrivals. We are entitled to a greater share of this kind of tourism, given our cultural stock. We shall do the same for the development of third-age tourism, a basic priority for our government. The authenticity, a quality particularly sought by the 2000 generation, the so-called'millennials', which are the tourists of the future, will be strengthened to highlight the choices the visitor has for a traditional accommodation, for a local food, for an authentic cultural or physiological activity, for the purchase of a traditional product, for acquaintance with local tradition and history. It's obvious from the actions that I described how far back we are in all of this. Fifthly - and if you want our ability to strengthen the tourism product - is social and environmental sensitivity. The upgrading of quality cannot stop in the size of the room or bed. It must embrace the energy footprint, care for those in need, the return of part of the value to the local society. This will be the vehicle of the sustainability of Greek tourism. So we'll build the comparative advantage in the decades to come. In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, all this will be in open dialogue with market operators. For this reason, we have already started informal meetings and are collecting market problems. We will continue with official meetings with all the presidencies of these bodies so that the problems that need to be addressed immediately before the start of the next tourist season. I would like to refer here to the 'Airbnb' point on which there has been a great deal of talk. For us in the Ministry of Tourism, short-term leases are part of the tourism product. This is not the case until now and for this the Ministry of Tourism was traditionally hostile to these leases. That is why I have already met with PASYDA, representing people who rent real estate. For us, short-term leases have been put on the tourism table. That position comes with rights. They'll ask for things, they'll say their problems, and we'll try to solve them. But they also come with obligations, because we must also ensure the quality of the tourist experience and its social footprint. Tax evasion and illegality cannot be tolerated. Ladies and gentlemen, I heard Mr Tsipras yesterday, and I would like to put aside his courage to talk about PPC, who is responsible for its reparation. But what seemed to me to be a complete lack of vision. He told us as a second-class accountant that, since we have 3.5% as a target, and since we are taking measures to relieve citizens, we must also take other secret measures to compensate for this loss. Mr Tsipras does not see that this gap will also be covered with our Ministry with development policies, with investment policies, support for tourism and support for development and investment.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u227	Mr. Aygeakis has the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u228	Thank you very much, Mr President. Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, we are starting a new parliamentary term, with optimism, vision and a plan. At the beginning of the new parliamentary term, I would also like to thank the institutions, from the Step of the House, my fellow citizens in Heraklion, who have honoured me with their vote for another term. I am also very grateful to the Prime Minister of our country, Mr Mitsotakis, for his confidence in me and honoured me with the portfolio of the State Secretary for Culture and Sport. Ladies and gentlemen, sport is an integral part of Greece's culture, the birthplace of the Olympic Games and the Olympic ideal. Sport is a huge cultural heritage. For the ancient Greeks the cultivation of the body is as important as the cultivation of the spirit. In classical Greece the two areas of human activity were sport for the body and music for the spirit. Of course, in modern Greece sport is under the protection and supreme supervision of the state, according to the Constitution. The Constitution itself in Article 12 requires the State to support sport, but also the possibility for citizens to join it freely through their organisations - clubs, sports associations, federations - in accordance with Article 16. Of course, sport has two more features: firstly, successes in sport unite us as a people. They create a sense of national pride, but also of unity, and that is something that we need as a country now more than ever. Secondly, sport brings the peoples of the whole world closer together. It creates a spirit of peace, of cooperation between the peoples and is a tool for even foreign policy. Sport, beyond our enormous cultural value, is also a great, but also a strong industry with many and important prospects. Ladies and gentlemen, in the New Democracy government programme we pay particular attention to sport. Our policies on sport are designed in conjunction with our policies on culture, education, health and the economy as a pillar of development and prosperity of our country's citizens. We want sport for the many, especially for the next generations. Ladies and gentlemen, in sport we have to catch the mito of fight from the outset, from what sport means, from the values of sport as a valuable social and cultural asset. Our policies on culture include both amateur and professional sport, as well as mass sport. Our plan for sport includes the following pillars: Support for professional and high-performance sport. Improving sport law, emphasising transparency, dealing with mismanagement and, of course, eliminating violence. Respect for the self-government of federations and the creation of a genuine and positive climate of cooperation. Control and supervision of the sports activities of professional sport. We are creating a transparent framework for the recognition of sports organisations. We emphasise the update of the federations' regulations. We are directly creating an online register of sports organisations. We will seek, by respecting budgetary matters, to increase the financing of federations with a major concern to raise and distinguish national groups of all ages, class and sex. Prevention and treatment of violence: We are determined to hand over the courtesy, races and every day of the week back to families, to the fan public, to make sport entertainment for young and old, strengthening the sense of security and protection. In cooperation with FIFA and UEFA we are setting up an immediate committee which, taking advantage of international experience and examining the specificities of our country, will provide solutions, of course, in cooperation with the Standing Committee on the Management of Violence and all those involved, such as the Ministry of Civil Protection and Justice. As far as the preconscious struggles are concerned, the problem exists. We can't ignore it or put it under the rug. On the other hand, we must look at its real dimensions and not make arbitrary genealogies. We are determined to deal with it drastically, but above all directly. We will use good knowledge, experience and modern methods to deal with this phenomenon. Already, at a meeting I had with the FIFA-UEFA step we discussed addressing this problem and we will soon be able to say much more. In the next few days, there will be meetings with the OSH, the ligaments and all the OSCE presidencies. We shall take all possible steps to ensure that there is joint consultation and joint initiatives. We are therefore considering dealing with the problem in other group professional sports as well. As far as anti-doping is concerned, we are institutionally strengthening and supporting the mission and work of the National Doping Combat Council (ESCU). It is our political priority to support pure sport against the modern scourge of enhanced over-performance. We have already agreed to set up a Scientific Committee to support the work of the ESRB. At the same time, we place particular emphasis on synergies with scientific institutional and self-government bodies, aimed at developing information and prevention programmes, particularly primary and secondary school pupils, as well as making better use of human resources. [[At this point, the closing bell of the speaking time of the Secretary-General is ringing.]] We support sport for everyone. Mass sports is our priority. We ensure proper sporting spaces to nurture citizens and especially young people in sport. We support and support the 'Sport for all' institution. We amplify sport in cooperation with the Ministry of Education. We are creating synergies with the OTAs, chambers, with legal persons, sports and social institutions, for the purpose of substantially strengthening mass sport. For example, we are ready for the first time this year for the distribution of positions for the prevention of Physical Education graduates of the "Suspension for All 2019-2020" programme. This also applies to the bathing teaching programme in the context of the Physical Education course in cooperation with the Ministry of Education. ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u367	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, it is an excellent honour and a special responsibility to address the National Representation for the first time today in the capacity of Prime Minister Kyriakos Mitsotakis to honour me by tabling the basic principles of our cultural programme in Greece tomorrow, in Greece, which seeks to put a crisis of excellence behind her. The cultural sector can make a decisive contribution to the implementation of the sustainable production model that the government wants to apply in the country: A strong Greece, which attracts domestic and foreign investment, creating new jobs with strong quality characteristics. We are investing in quality and excellence, concepts which have been systematically ignored in recent years. We are experiencing the fourth industrial revolution, which is not only about changing technologies, about changing social relations, production standards, and the balance between economic and intellectual capital. The prevalence of artificial intelligence and technology poses new economic, social and cultural challenges. In this environment we must plan and implement our policies. It sounds common, but it's true. Culture is our great comparative advantage. However, the country has not capitalised its cultural heritage and the dynamics of modern creation, so as to gain a greater share of capital at international level. In the context of softness, cultural policies contribute decisively to rebranding, such as the international term, namely the redefinition of the country's identity. We are dealing with culture as a whole as an export product. We are adopting international good practices. Events on cultural heritage promptly mobilise international interest. A similar interest must also be created for modern Greek culture in the international environment, whether it is the artistic arts or the literature. The dynamics exist. Our creators are the best ambassadors to the image of the country. We are developing the necessary synergies with the appropriate ministries so that Greece can become an international pole of cultural education and creativity. In 2021 we will host for the first time the leading cultural institution of Europe, the Capital of Culture, in Elefsina, the birthplace of Aeschylus, the city whose modern history is linked to the industrial development of the country. Elefsina's success is a matter of national importance. With the responsibility of the previous government, both at the level of operation of the entity and at the level of the infrastructure, we are far from desirable. It is our priority, in cooperation with the co-legislative ministries and local government, to speed up procedures and to provide any assistance in order to complete the two major infrastructure projects: the old "IRIS" plant and the Oilworking plant, but also to overcome administrative malfunctions. Ladies and gentlemen, culture as a social asset, which means that it is legally accessible to all, as a body of strong symbolism and values, as a field of fertile dialogue and interaction with the different, as a source of the creation of social capital, is a key factor in social integration, social cohesion and harmonious coexistence. Our aim is to strengthen equal access to culture and to involve culture in the social inclusion of vulnerable groups. The first measures are to extend the special actions for vulnerable groups already implemented in museums and archaeological sites. Secondly, the association of culture policies with public health policies, obviously through synergies with the Ministry of Health in internationally applied standards. Thirdly, the issue of a digital cultural card for free or with reduced access to cultural sites, so that the unthinkable 21st century requirement of showing printed family status certificates by beneficiaries can be stopped. Ladies and gentlemen, culture is the fourth pillar of sustainable development. The government treats the sector as a strategic development resource. The cultural stock of each region creates added value and is not meant to be treated as a brake on development, but cannot be used to prevent investment. According to the existing institutional framework, the protection of cultural heritage is included in the objectives of any level of development, environmental and spatial planning. Only if the protection is combined with growth can the necessary resources be provided for research, conservation, promotion, promotion of the cultural stock, prevention of risks to climate change. Substantial protection must be part of a development dynamic. Ideas and strangles are only at the expense of cultural goods. Today we must constantly prove in practice our will for reforms that will make our society sustainable. Through the cases of the Greek, investment programs at the port of Piraeus, the airport at Kastelli, the efficient and efficient operation of the state mechanism must be demonstrated. The use of cultural goods affects both the symbolic and the real values of the development of the regions, namely the real economy. We know that the EUR 1 investment in cultural projects is a five-year horizon of EUR 3,44, that is, the investment is more than tripled. Culture is recognised as a distinct economic sector using resources, produces products and services, creates income and jobs. It creates measurable positive external economies, due to its interconnection with a variety of sectors and services, including cultural tourism and cultural creative industries, which are the most emerging economic sector. They include, among others, advertising, architecture, craftsmanship, design, fashion, cinema, music, artistic arts, publications, software, electronic games. They are essentially a tool for upgrading human creativity that is expected to be the main lever of economic growth in the future. Our aim is firstly to support and promote the cultural and creative industries in the country and to interconnect them with other key sectors of the economy, secondly to resolve the problem of the unequal distribution of cultural and creative production, employment and the number of enterprises in the thirteen regions of the country. Cultural tourism in the context of the economy of experience invests in cultural routes, enriches the tourist product with inputs and interoperability from the cultural creative industries. Cultural routes provide new readings to the monuments, rescue and promote intangible heritage. They set up networks offering work to creative people and innovative small and medium-sized enterprises in the wider cultural sector, from arts to gastronomy. Greece has an important advantage, the possibility of highlighting and exploiting its monumental wealth through its association with modern creation. This relationship enriches and differentiates the tourist product qualitatively and aesthetically. Our aim is to contribute to the urban and economic recovery of historic towns. Especially for Athens, the renaissance of the National Archaeological Museum, which was mentioned yesterday by the Prime Minister, has been launched. The National Archaeological Museum and the National Metsovio Polytechnics create an ultra-local cultural pole, they conceive. The museum is expanding underground in order to ensure the necessary resources for the proper exposure of its valuable collections which make it internationally unique. It connects underground and exploits the infrastructures of “AKROPOL”, which will operate, as expected from 2014, as a cultural thermostat of contemporary creation. It develops synergies with the Architecture School of Metsovio Polytechnics, which remains in its premises. The resources for the studies, as the Prime Minister said, have been guaranteed by the Stavros Niarchos Foundation. However, it does not mean regeneration of the centre of Athens without the immediate completion and reopening of the National Gallery, but also without the function of at last the National Museum of Contemporary Art. Major museum infrastructure, state and private, requires certification. We are directly applying the museum recognition system - which has been cancerous since 2018 - and we are expanding it. We are moving on to modernise the institutional framework concerning the operation of public museums by creating legal persons governed by public law, where necessary. Museums remain state-owned, but operate autonomously, apparently applying the Ministry's museum policy. The sustainability of cultural structures is directly intertwined by their adaptation to digital technology. It is necessary to review the policy implemented with a view to free access to knowledge and culture. Our immediate priority is to put forward a proposal for the organised disposal of cultural content and the application of Community legislation on open data without asterisks. The role of the State should concern exclusively with the supervision and incorporation of European legislation into national law. Our concern is that the operation of the Ministry of Culture and Sport should be governed by horizontal principles aimed at guaranteeing public value.

I am now committed to you and the Greek people for a gradual reduction in the problems involved. The third axis of the program is care for the most vulnerable, namely the provision of an appointment with doctors within 24 hours, the distribution of drugs for the severely ill at home and a series of measures that will improve the quality of life of our fellow citizens. Another axis of our programme is the modern and efficient management of the health system. We are suffering from this, namely the e-government of the health system, the evaluation of the quality of health services by patients, the reduction of the time required for prescribing to receive treatment and the reduction of private expenditure gradually until it reaches the average of the advanced countries of southern Europe. Ladies and gentlemen, there is a great need for structural changes, as well as structural reforms. We have a health system with obvious signs of old age. It is also necessary - and I think this is also clear today - to change culture and attitudes in what it has to do with its operation. We have the intention and we are determined as a new political leadership, with the commands of the Prime Minister of the country, Mr Mitsotakis, to shake the waters and bring about a plan of change of culture and attitudes so that public health and human beings can be served. From this step, however, I sincerely ask you to support the active support of the entire community involved in the provision of health services. I would ask the citizens, obviously, to do the same. Public health needs us all, and no one needs more, ladies and gentlemen. And doctors and nurses and managers, and citizens, I think, are clearly worthy of better public health. However, I call for everyone to be supported so that we can promote the necessary changes required in the health system to serve the citizen with dignity. I think it is a matter of culture, it is a matter of humanity and it goes beyond the limits of any political party. Our strategy at the Ministry of Health, in order to achieve all of this, is structured in the following pillars: Firstly, the development of public health as an absolute priority for national health policy. Secondly, providing adequate, safe, high quality and effective primary care. Thirdly, administrative restructuring of hospitals and redesign the way in which health services are provided. Fourthly, the development of medieval care structures. Fifth, providing holistic care to people with mental health problems. Sixth, assessment of health technology, in order to provide the most effective treatments at the lowest possible cost. Ladies and gentlemen, our indiscriminate principles in the implementation of this plan are equality, social justice, the efficient use of resources and the improvement of clinical practice. That is why we are changing the financing model of the system so that it is sustainable and rationally distributed and used. We are also strengthening the pillar of public health and implementing a national anti-discrimination plan. Our principle in the implementation of this project is, without any doubt, more social justice. With them we are directly promoting synergies and partnerships with the private sector, by applying modern financial models, particularly with regard to the replacement of biomedical equipment and by developing new services in order to transform the system from hospital to anthropocentric. We are signing a three-year cooperation agreement with all health institutions, taking into account their own proposals. We will listen to them all carefully and take account of what they have to say and add to the health programme. We are actually taking structural measures to contain and minimise the impact of the market correction mechanism, i.e. rebate and clawback. I'd have a lot to say about what we inherit in this part, but they're Programmatic Declarations and they're not here. We are making use of the possibilities of medical tourism and we are creating a friendly environment for attracting research funds in the field of health technology. I must also briefly mention the development of healthy ageing programmes for the population, the strengthening of access for vulnerable groups to health services, and of course, as the Prime Minister has announced, the end to the tails of shame in the EEA pharmacies. We want pharmacies to be a health and information hub for citizens. We firmly believe that our only interventions in the existing institutional framework must be to provide incentives for tax and insurance, to create associations, to care for the pharmacies of remote areas and to modernise the operation of the Pan-Hellenic Pharmacopoeia and other pharmaceutical associations. At the same time, we extend the vaccination coverage of children and the elderly and start war against the unreasonable use of antibiotics. We are implementing pre-symptomatic cancer control and prevention programmes for the early diagnosis and the best possible treatment of the disease. We are strengthening and modernising our hospitals with state-of-the-art technologies. We thus offer upgraded services to citizens without the same charge and send a message to everyone that Greece is now a pole of investment in the health sector. We're reconstructing patient flow procedures in hospitals. We manage the developed IF beds with the necessary staff. We proceed directly to the recruitment of absolute priority, the Prime Minister announced, first of all, for nursing staff. We develop the Autonomous Emergency Departments, the MDGs, which we interconnect with the EMCDDA. We are strengthening the strategic role of EEAS. The EEA will be modernised with predetermined quality and cost criteria combined with the real health needs of local populations. Ladies and gentlemen, it is proved and we know firsthand that Greek citizens are paying too much for health in relation to other Europeans and we know that the quality of health services in Greece is lower than in most European countries. We, today, from the Step forward of the Greek Parliament, are committed to ensuring better quality of health services for all citizens and at a lower cost than they do. We are ambitious to build a new health system that will focus on human beings, because as Hippocrates said "it is impossible for him to know the medicine that he does not know exactly what man is". ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u412	Mr Papathanase, State Secretary for Development and Investment, has the floor. Four speakers, MPs, Mrs Canelli, Mr Charakopoulos, Mr Heitas, Mrs Skondra and then a Minister will follow. Come on, Mr. Papathanasis, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u413	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the relaunching of our economy and the safeguarding of a dynamic development track requires a state of modern, flexible and friendly to entrepreneurship and investment. At the same time, it requires safeguards that defend the public interest and serve collective and social good. This was the aim of Kyriakos Mitsotakis and this objective we are serving at the Ministry of Development and Investment. We are ready to promote bold reforms, which relate to simplifying licensing procedures, more efficient market surveillance, but also to reducing administrative burdens for all businesses. In more detail, our philosophy of reform in industry focuses on the almost absolute shift of ex ante control over authorisation to post-control. This is always based on risk assessment and the use of new administrative tools and technologies. The State trusts the trading investor and businessman. So, first of all, we are reforming and fully simplifying the business environment, in the context of enhanced supervision in three pillars. The first pillar: we simplify and regulate the licensing procedures in a uniform and coherent manner for all economic activities in Greece, as is, for example, the alternative licensing based on standards, those under Law 4262, the acceleration of logistics licensing, authorisation by private operators and other simplifications. Secondly, we are promoting and implementing a new institutional framework for the supervision and control of economic activities and the purchase of products. Third pillar: We develop the integrated OPC-AS information system, which will be entrepreneurship-friendly, supporting the digitisation of licensing and supervision processes. Secondly, we are reviewing the categorisation to the extent of nuisance of industrial and craft activities. Thirdly, we are revising the provisions of Law 3325/2005 on the status of establishment, operation, expansion and modernisation for the industry in Attica. Fourthly, we are reforming the institutional framework for the establishment, organisation and operation of business parks. This reform is absolutely necessary and necessary because only 5% of the recorded activities operate within organised receptors. We are further strengthening relations between the management body and the establishment. Fifthly, we are improving the institutional framework governing the technical professions, because the existing regulations are inconsistent with current developments. Sixthly, we are recommending a national board for industry that is necessary to formulate a modern national industrial and manufacturing policy. And finally, seven, for industry, we are upgrading the national accreditation and certification system with many benefits for industry and market surveillance. In the area of trade now and the consumer: firstly, we eliminate bureaucracy by simplifying procedures for businesses in their trade with GEMI services. The GFCM is interconnected with public and other registries and ceases to waste resources by transferring documents between public services. Secondly, we are speeding up the implementation of Law4601 on corporate transformations and conversions of businesses so that we can release forces to relaunch the economy. Thirdly, we are restructured the state control mechanism for dealing with the illegal trade and the paramilitary economy, which is thriving. The action of illegal networks for the promotion and marketing of products on the Greek market is a scourge for businesses that fight for their survival. Fourthly, we protect the interests of the consumer with a comprehensive and modern information mechanism for the recording and publication of the prices of goods that form the market basket and affect family income. Fifthly, we ensure transparency in consumer e-commerce through the development of a control action plan for suppliers' websites to establish the application of consumer legislation. Sixthly, we are amending the legal framework governing the operation of the debtors information companies, collectives and dues, with a view to enhancing privacy and banking confidentiality. Seventh, we upgrade the host and consumer complaint management centre, through a mechanism for consolidating and interoperability of software systems. Eighth, we are modernising and strengthening the national central purchasing authority, public procurement, with a view to transparency of transactions, healthy competition between suppliers, facilitating access to small and medium-sized enterprises and creating economies of scale for the benefit of public finances. Finally, we are completing the Green Public Procurement Action Plan, including environmental criteria in the technical specifications of goods or services purchased by the public to reduce the environmental footprint. Ladies and gentlemen, on 7 July Greece looked forward. We are working intensively to respond directly and effectively to the great challenge of strong and sustainable development. We open the doors of Greece to the global investment community. The aim is to run quickly to bring wealth to the country, new and better paid jobs, tangible results, which improve the lives of the Greeks. Kyriakos Mitsotakis and the new government know very well that Greeks cannot wait. We respond with a rapid implementation of the reform programme, which will escalate growth, transform the country into a pro-investment site and give financial space for prosperity. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] I shall conclude, Mr President. The Ministry of Development and Investment is invited to play a leading role in the new page that we have already opened and that is what we are doing. We turn the state from manoeuvre to a private initiative, we seek to create a new investment climate, we look forward to a new investment and entrepreneurial culture. Ladies and gentlemen, we have complete confidence in the creative powers of our country. We trust the healthy entrepreneurship and above all the strength, skills, creativity and workability of the Greeks. We can make it. We can bring strong growth and self-powered Greece. Thank you.
