ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-21-S1-commons.u442	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, health is, together with education and the economy, a tripartite of social, economic and human development. Our health, health care and development strategy is fully synchronised with the strategy of the World Health Organisation, identified by the Alma Ata Declaration and more recently Astana, aiming at health development based on the couple of primary care and public health. We are aiming to add value to health by improving the quality of life associated with it, aimed at increasing not only the years of life but especially the years of well-being that can be achieved with primary care, prevention, pre-symptomatic control, emphasis on family care and control of behavioural and social risk factors for health. We aim to add value to medical care through the efficient use of human, material and economic resources through the operational improvement of hospitals and the introduction of professional management to develop space to improve the position of all health professionals, as well as the response to the expectations of fellow citizens. We are also aiming at the development value of the health sector by improving care, research and education production and thereby creating jobs and contributing to GDP growth. Ladies and gentlemen, the health policy in the country is changing. The focus on public health in the light of the tools offered by the concept of new public health is our main strategy. From the close management and management of health care services we are moving to a wider framework for managing and controlling major health risk factors. Among the risk factors that are prominent in the country, according to the findings of national and international organisations, smoking and obesity is taking place. We therefore give priority in the coming period to the integrated policies for managing and controlling these risks. We are also not neglecting the issue of the already chronic illnesses. Today in the country about four out of ten adults suffer from chronic illness. The proper management of chronic diseases can ensure years of good health and lead, on the one hand, to efficient management of resources and, on the other, to a reduction in the individual burden on patients for health costs. We provide comprehensive care for patients with chronic illnesses in specialized primary health care structures, starting with diabetes and hypertension. We strengthen the role of general family medicine in Greece and primary care. First contact healthcare professionals, doctors, community nurses, social workers and so on will function as patient care professionals in the long run of a disease. We're introducing accountability. The Ministry, hospitals and all health institutions must be accountable. For reasons of transparency and also knowledge of how taxpayers' money is being used, the publicity of operators' operational and economic data on a regular basis will be mandatory. The priority is also the adoption of systems to strengthen patients in decision-making. The citizens will have all the qualitative and quantitative information at their disposal. To this end - we have already announced in our government programme - we are moving directly towards the implementation and functioning of the quality assurance principle of health services, an authority that will assess the bodies, publish results and control health providers. The citizens themselves will also assess the quality of health services, as is the case in every modern state with tools to measure their experience in contact with the health system and reports of outcomes. As we know the problems that the public encounters in contact with the system and the perception of their health and satisfaction and response from the health system, we will be able to deal with and plan better health services. Each euro which spends the system will be recorded. We respect and value the last euro of the available health funds, which we will try to increase, so that we reach the average public health expenditure between OECD countries. In this respect, we are changing the view of compensation for the system. The state won't just compensate services. The model of value-based compensation, i.e. based on the results obtained from the care provided, will pass through the whole health sector. Especially in medicines for those categories that are possible, and especially for the cost, we will make the most of modern trading and compensation techniques, and we will apply target scanning systems to identify and prioritise the upcoming new technologies and their impact on the system. At the same time, we are moving towards the establishment of the national health assessment and excellence agency in clinical practice. All of this is not possible without the digital revolution in health. Existing databases and data systems, such as the electronic prescribing system, will be strengthened by both new recording tools, such as the complete patient file, and magnified management techniques and tools for analysing them. We are boosting research, not only in the field of clinical trials but in the whole research on health technology, providing incentives for health companies to invest in our country. We are exploiting the production resources and the worthy of public hospitals through the conclusion of contracts for the provision of services on a fee basis from the private insurance market. The sums that can come from and enter the public health system are hundreds of millions of euros. We ensure the public nature of the health system and the timely access of every citizen to health services, cooperating with the private sector in the field of health services. Public-private partnerships ensure efficiency in the system, but also that no hospital stays without equipment of modern diagnostic and invasive technology and no fellow citizen will be troubled in the runways of the National Health System. In conclusion, I would like to stress once again this: we will take care of every euro that is spent on producing the greatest possible value for the citizens who need health services. Besides, the health system is designed to serve the needs of every citizen. The opportunity for the country is the last: either now or never. Our motto "better public health for all Greeks" is not a simple slogan, it is our deep conviction that we must respond to a modern health system for citizens. With a compass of public and social interests and the confidence of the Greek Parliament, we move forward so that all our fellow citizens have a modern, public, fair and effective health system at their disposal. Thank you very much.

The vision I will serve is on a line: The creation of an extroverted Greece that aims to stimulate the Greek economy and improve the quality of life of each Greek and every Greek. Ladies and gentlemen, it is a vision ambitious with a plannary pragmatic. But it is also a government priority. If, then, this is the vision I serve, what are our strategic goals? Quite simply, the exercise of effective economic diplomacy, the support of Greek exports and the expansion of the export base, the attraction of investments regardless of size, the modernisation of public administration structures to facilitate investors and exporters and, finally, the redefinition and international building of the image of extrovert Greece. Given the strategic direction I have mentioned, we will take the following actions. Firstly, we are upgrading and modernising economic diplomacy at bilateral and multilateral level. We are creating a single and enhanced extroversion body, merging all relevant agencies and services to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, with a General Secretariat of International Economic Relations and Extroversion, with a view to better coordination, more efficient administration and the existence of a single, clear and strong voice. I am referring, of course, to the ENTERPRISE GREECE, the Export Credit Insurance Agency, our Permanent Representation to the OECD, the Office for Economic and Commercial Counsellor of Geneva and the full utilization of the structures of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, which extends to the whole network and external authorities to a hundred and twenty-seven embassies and generally consulates and fifty-nine economic and commercial affairs offices. Secondly, we create a real one stop shop, which will be close to businesses to solve problems, simplify procedures and facilitate investment, both Greek and foreign, in our country. We are, of course, going to work closely with ministries and bodies, so that with appropriate reforms in the national legislative and fiscal framework for the business, the right investment environment can be created. We will show the partners, the competent international organisations and the international business community the new environment with honesty, highlighting our country's new pro-investment perspective. Thirdly, we are putting an end to general activity in export promotion and we are now moving in a targeted way. We are setting the priorities of the markets and sectors of the economy, giving incentives, appropriate information and education, where necessary, together with tools for extroverting Greek businesses to offer products and services competitively at international level. Fourthly, we are drawing up policies for strengthening business schemes in the field of research and innovation, attracting prominent world leaders and, at the same time, promoting and supporting young people at the beginning - start up it is called - entrepreneurship. Fifthly, we take advantage of Greece's geographical position, which is one of our comparative advantages. It is one of the features that can and support the extroversion of the country. Our goal is to create the terms and conditions so that the Greek market in products, services and human resources can become an international business hub. We now live in a global village and technology enables us to meet the new challenges. Sixth, we are building the new upgraded image of extroverted Greece at international level, but also internally. We aim to recover the lost ground because of the protracted economic crisis, but also the bad, often twisted image of our country. Thus, we are already planning the architecture of the image of a modern extrovert country. Some call it national brand, but it's actually much more than that, because it's not about marketing, but it's about the country's position in the world. It is the architecture that consists of all the important elements of the present state of our country, all of which together compose what we want to be, a modern, multidimensional, single, effective voice and image of the new Greece in the global landscape. Of course, we will develop the closest possible cooperation with the Office of Prime Minister, the Ministries and the Ministers, the Secretary-General, the officials and officials of the public administration, as well as with social partners, bodies, associations of exporters, chambers and other expressions of civil society and business.
